> DEVELOPER

I need you to review the refactor commits from the past hour on this branch to assess whether they're actually improving the codebase. Look at the diffs and the resulting file structure.

Focus on these refactor commits:
1. 631054d refactor: split typescript pattern detector modules
2. 721aed4 refactor: split typescript smell helper utilities
3. 5e78423 refactor: split typescript smell detector internals
4. a53b18c refactor: split typescript command wrappers from registry
5. bf6d328 refactor: reduce complexity in tree context smell detector
6. 8e02f59 refactor: split python AST node smell detectors
7. 3edc2d3 refactor: split python mutable state detector by concern
8. 23d6a48 refactor: extract python dict-key visitor helper logic
9. 20d6f4e refactor: split python dict-key detector internals
10. a22d604 refactor: split csharp deps support helpers by concern
11. b05a8fd refactor: split treesitter language specs into grouped modules
12. 0638949 refactor: split treesitter import resolvers by language family
13. 78320f2 refactor: split treesitter complexity metrics by concern
14. 18a8ec6 refactor: extract lang runtime state accessors
15. 58e0841 refactor: split generic plugin assembly into focused modules
16. 303038d refactor: remove re-export facades and backward-compat aliases

For each commit, run `git show --stat <hash>` and for the ones that look interesting/concerning, look at the actual diff with `git show <hash>`. 

Key questions to answer for each:
- Is the split justified? (Was the original file actually too large?)
- Are the new files coherent units, or just arbitrary chunks?
- Did the split introduce unnecessary indirection or boilerplate?
- Are there any signs of over-engineering (tiny files with just 1-2 functions, excessive __init__.py re-exports)?

Also check the current state of some of the split directories to see if we've ended up with file bloat:
- `ls -la desloppify/languages/typescript/` and subdirs
- `ls -la desloppify/languages/python/` and subdirs  
- `ls -la desloppify/languages/treesitter/` and subdirs
- `ls -la desloppify/languages/` overall

Give me a honest assessment. Don't sugarcoat - if this is just busywork splitting that creates more files without real benefit, say so.

```
-"""Pattern consistency analysis: flag areas with competing approaches to the same problem.
+"""Compatibility facade for TypeScript pattern consistency detection."""
 
-Key design principle: Complementary patterns (layers) are NOT fragmentation.
-- handleError wraps console.error + toast.error → having all 3 is healthy
-- useQuery (reads) + useMutation (writes) + supabase.from (one-offs) → different layers
-- Loader2 (spinner) + Skeleton (placeholder) → different UX contexts
-
-Only COMPETING patterns (multiple approaches to the same decision) are flagged:
-- useAutoSaveSettings vs usePersistentToolState vs useToolSettings → same problem,
-  different tradeoffs. An area using 2+ suggests fragmentation or migration debt.
-
-All families are included in the census (raw `detect patterns` command shows everything).
-Only competing families produce issues for the scan pipeline.
-"""

-from collections import defaultdict

 )

-#
-# type="competing": These patterns solve the SAME problem with different approaches.
-#   Fragmentation = area uses >= threshold patterns from this family.
-#   Outlier = area uses a pattern adopted by <10% of areas.
-#
-# type="complementary": These patterns are intentional layers, not alternatives.
```

```
-"""AST-based complexity signals using tree-sitter."""
+"""AST-based complexity signals using tree-sitter.
 
-from __future__ import annotations
-
-import logging
-from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Any, Callable
-
-from . import PARSE_INIT_ERRORS
-from ._cache import _PARSE_CACHE
-from ._extractors import _get_parser
-
-if TYPE_CHECKING:
-    from desloppify.languages._framework.treesitter import TreeSitterLangSpec
-
-logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
-
-ComputeFn = Callable[[str, list[str]], tuple[int, str] | None]
-"""Signature for complexity signal compute functions.
-
-Each factory below returns a closure matching this type:
-``(content, lines, *, _filepath="") -> (count, label) | None``.
+Compatibility facade that re-exports metric builders from focused modules.
 """
 
+from __future__ import annotations
 
-def _ensure_parser(
-    cache: dict[str, Any],
-    spec: TreeSitterLangSpec,
-    *,
-    with_query: bool = False,
-) -> bool:
-    """Lazily initialise parser (and optionally a function query) into *cache*.
-
-    Returns ``True`` if the cache is ready, ``False`` on initialisation
-    failure.  Callers should ``return None`` on ``False``.
-    """
-    if "parser" in cache:
-        return True
-    try:
-        p, lang = _get_parser(spec.grammar)
-        cache["parser"] = p
-        cache["language"] = lang
-        if with_query:
-            from ._extractors import _make_query
-
-            cache["query"] = _make_query(lang, spec.function_query)
-    except PARSE_INIT_ERRORS as exc:
-        logger.debug("tree-sitter init failed: %s", exc)
-        return False
-    return True
-
-# Node types that increase nesting depth (cross-language superset).
-_NESTING_NODE_TYPES = frozenset({
-    # Conditionals
-    "if_statement", "if_expression", "if_let_expression",
-    "else_clause", "elif_clause",
-    # Loops
-    "for_statement", "for_expression", "for_in_statement",
-    "while_statement", "while_expression", "do_statement",
-    "loop_expression",
-    # Error handling
-    "try_statement", "try_expression", "catch_clause", "rescue",
-    "except_clause",
-    # Branching
-    "switch_statement", "switch_expression", "match_expression",
-    "case_clause", "match_arm",
-    # Blocks that indicate nesting
-    "with_statement", "with_clause",
-    # Closures/lambdas
-    "lambda_expression", "closure_expression",
-})
-
-
-def compute_nesting_depth_ts(
-    filepath: str, spec: TreeSitterLangSpec, parser, language
-) -> int | None:
-    """Compute max nesting depth using AST traversal.
-
-    Walks the parse tree and counts depth of nested control-flow nodes.
-    Returns max depth found in the file, or None if file can't be parsed.
-    """
-    cached = _PARSE_CACHE.get_or_parse(filepath, parser, spec.grammar)
-    if cached is None:
-        return None
-    _source, tree = cached
-
-    # Iterative DFS with depth tracking.
-    max_depth = 0
-    # Stack: (node, current_nesting_depth)
-    stack: list[tuple[object, int]] = [(tree.root_node, 0)]
-
-    while stack:
-        node, depth = stack.pop()
-        if node.type in _NESTING_NODE_TYPES:
-            depth += 1
-            if depth > max_depth:
-                max_depth = depth
-
-        for i in range(node.child_count - 1, -1, -1):
-            stack.append((node.children[i], depth))
-
-    return max_depth
-
-
-def make_nesting_depth_compute(spec: TreeSitterLangSpec) -> ComputeFn:
-    """Create a compute function for nesting depth using tree-sitter.
-
-    Returns a callable matching ComplexitySignal.compute signature:
-    (content: str, lines: list[str]) -> (count, label) | None
-    """
-    # Cache parser/language per spec to avoid repeated lookups.
-    _cached_parser: dict[str, Any] = {}
-
-    def compute(content: str, lines: list[str], *, _filepath: str = "") -> tuple[int, str] | None:
-        if not _filepath:
-            return None
-
-        if not _ensure_parser(_cached_parser, spec):
-            return None
-
-        depth = compute_nesting_depth_ts(
-            _filepath, spec, _cached_parser["parser"], _cached_parser["language"]
-        )
-        if depth is None or depth <= 0:
-            return None
-        return depth, f"nesting depth {depth}"
-
-    return compute
-
-
-def make_long_functions_compute(spec: TreeSitterLangSpec) -> ComputeFn:
-    """Create a compute function for long functions using tree-sitter.
-
-    Flags the longest function in a file if it exceeds the threshold.
-    Returns a callable matching ComplexitySignal.compute signature.
-    """
-    from ._extractors import _run_query, _unwrap_node
-
-    _cached_parser: dict[str, Any] = {}
-
-    def compute(content: str, lines: list[str], *, _filepath: str = "") -> tuple[int, str] | None:
-        if not _filepath:
-            return None
-
-        if not _ensure_parser(_cached_parser, spec, with_query=True):
-            return None
-
-        parser = _cached_parser["parser"]
-        query = _cached_parser["query"]
-
-        cached = _PARSE_CACHE.get_or_parse(_filepath, parser, spec.grammar)
-        if cached is None:
-            return None
-        _source, tree = cached
-
-        matches = _run_query(query, tree.root_node)
-        max_loc = 0
-        for _pattern_idx, captures in matches:
-            func_node = _unwrap_node(captures.get("func"))
-            if not func_node:
-                continue
-            loc = func_node.end_point[0] - func_node.start_point[0] + 1
-            if loc > max_loc:
-                max_loc = loc
-
-        if max_loc <= 0:
-            return None
-        return max_loc, f"longest function {max_loc} LOC"
-
-    return compute
-
-
-# ── Cyclomatic complexity per function ────────────────────────
-
-# Node types that represent branching decisions (adds 1 to cyclomatic complexity).
-_BRANCHING_NODE_TYPES = frozenset({
-    "if_statement", "if_expression", "if_let_expression",
-    "elif_clause", "else_if",
-    "for_statement", "for_expression", "for_in_statement",
-    "while_statement", "while_expression", "do_statement",
-    "loop_expression",
-    "case_clause", "match_arm",
-    "catch_clause", "rescue", "except_clause",
-    "ternary_expression", "conditional_expression",
-    # Logical operators (short-circuit evaluation).
-    "binary_expression",  # checked for && / || below
-})
-
-_LOGICAL_OPS = frozenset({"&&", "||", "and", "or"})
-
-
-def _count_decisions(node) -> int:
-    """Count decision points in a subtree for cyclomatic complexity."""
-    count = 0
-    stack = [node]
-    while stack:
-        n = stack.pop()
-        if n.type in _BRANCHING_NODE_TYPES:
-            if n.type == "binary_expression":
-                # Only count if the operator is a logical operator.
-                for child in n.children:
-                    if child.type in _LOGICAL_OPS or (
-                        child.child_count == 0 and child.text and
-                        (child.text if isinstance(child.text, str) else child.text.decode("utf-8", "replace"))
-                        in _LOGICAL_OPS
-                    ):
-                        count += 1
-                        break
-            else:
-                count += 1
-        for i in range(n.child_count - 1, -1, -1):
-            stack.append(n.children[i])
-    return count
-
-
-def make_cyclomatic_complexity_compute(spec: TreeSitterLangSpec) -> ComputeFn:
-    """Create a compute function for max cyclomatic complexity per function.
-
-    Cyclomatic complexity = 1 + number of decision points (if/for/while/case/catch/&&/||).
-    Returns the max CC across all functions in the file.
-    """
-    from ._extractors import _run_query, _unwrap_node
-
-    _cached_parser: dict[str, Any] = {}
-
-    def compute(content: str, lines: list[str], *, _filepath: str = "") -> tuple[int, str] | None:
-        if not _filepath:
-            return None
-
-        if not _ensure_parser(_cached_parser, spec, with_query=True):
-            return None
-
-        parser = _cached_parser["parser"]
-        query = _cached_parser["query"]
-
-        cached = _PARSE_CACHE.get_or_parse(_filepath, parser, spec.grammar)
-        if cached is None:
-            return None
-        _source, tree = cached
-
-        matches = _run_query(query, tree.root_node)
-        max_cc = 0
-        for _pattern_idx, captures in matches:
-            func_node = _unwrap_node(captures.get("func"))
-            if not func_node:
-                continue
-            cc = 1 + _count_decisions(func_node)
-            if cc > max_cc:
-                max_cc = cc
-
-        if max_cc <= 1:
-            return None
-        return max_cc, f"cyclomatic complexity {max_cc}"
-
-    return compute
-
-
-# ── Long parameter lists ─────────────────────────────────────
-
```

```
-def resolve_fsharp_import(import_text: str, source_file: str, scan_path: str) -> str | None:

-def _camel_to_snake(name: str) -> str:

-def resolve_r_import(import_text: str, source_file: str, scan_path: str) -> str | None:
-    """Resolve R source() calls to local files.
-
-    library() and require() load external CRAN packages — return None.
-    source() loads local R scripts — resolve to path.
-    """
-    if not import_text:
-        return None
-
-    text = import_text.strip("\"'")
-
-    # Only source() calls reference local files.
-    # library("pkg") and require("pkg") are external packages.
-    if not text.endswith((".R", ".r")):
-        return None
-
-    # Resolve relative to source file directory.
-    base = os.path.dirname(source_file)
-    candidate = os.path.normpath(os.path.join(base, text))
-    if os.path.isfile(candidate):
-        return candidate
-
-    # Try relative to scan path (R/ subdirectory is standard for packages).
-    for src_root in [".", "R"]:
-        candidate = os.path.join(scan_path, src_root, text)
-        if os.path.isfile(candidate):
-            return candidate
-
-    return None
```

```
1→"""Shared parser/bootstrap helpers for tree-sitter complexity signals."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import logging
     6→from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Any, Callable
     7→
     8→from . import PARSE_INIT_ERRORS
     9→from ._extractors import _get_parser
    10→
    11→if TYPE_CHECKING:
    12→    from desloppify.languages._framework.treesitter import TreeSitterLangSpec
    13→
    14→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    15→
    16→ComputeFn = Callable[[str, list[str]], tuple[int, str] | None]
    17→"""Signature for complexity signal compute functions.
    18→
    19→Each factory returns a closure with this effective call shape:
    20→``(content, lines, *, _filepath=\"\") -> (count, label) | None``.
    21→"""
    22→
    23→
    24→def _ensure_parser(
    25→    cache: dict[str, Any],
    26→    spec: TreeSitterLangSpec,
    27→    *,
    28→    with_query: bool = False,
    29→) -> bool:
    30→    """Lazily initialise parser (and optionally function query) into *cache*."""
    31→    if "parser" in cache:
    32→        return True
    33→    try:
    34→        parser, lang = _get_parser(spec.grammar)
    35→        cache["parser"] = parser
    36→        cache["language"] = lang
    37→        if with_query:
    38→            from ._extractors import _make_query
    39→
    40→            cache["query"] = _make_query(lang, spec.function_query)
    41→    except PARSE_INIT_ERRORS as exc:
    42→        logger.debug("tree-sitter init failed: %s", exc)
    43→        return False
    44→    return True
    45→
```

```
1→"""Callback/closure nesting metric for tree-sitter complexity."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Any
     6→
     7→from ._cache import _PARSE_CACHE
     8→from ._complexity_shared import ComputeFn, _ensure_parser
     9→
    10→if TYPE_CHECKING:
    11→    from desloppify.languages._framework.treesitter import TreeSitterLangSpec
    12→
    13→
    14→_CLOSURE_NODE_TYPES = frozenset(
    15→    {
    16→        "arrow_function",
    17→        "function_expression",
    18→        "function",
    19→        "lambda_expression",
    20→        "closure_expression",
    21→        "lambda",
    22→        "anonymous_function",
    23→        "block_argument",
    24→        "func_literal",
    25→    }
    26→)
    27→
    28→
    29→def make_callback_depth_compute(spec: TreeSitterLangSpec) -> ComputeFn:
    30→    """Build a complexity compute callback for callback/closure nesting depth."""
    31→    _cached_parser: dict[str, Any] = {}
    32→
    33→    def compute(content: str, lines: list[str], *, _filepath: str = "") -> tuple[int, str] | None:
    34→        del content, lines
    35→        if not _filepath:
    36→            return None
    37→        if not _ensure_parser(_cached_parser, spec):
    38→            return None
    39→
    40→        parser = _cached_parser["parser"]
    41→        cached = _PARSE_CACHE.get_or_parse(_filepath, parser, spec.grammar)
    42→        if cached is None:
    43→            return None
    44→        _source, tree = cached
    45→
    46→        max_depth = 0
    47→        stack: list[tuple[object, int]] = [(tree.root_node, 0)]
    48→        while stack:
    49→            node, depth = stack.pop()
    50→            if node.type in _CLOSURE_NODE_TYPES:
    51→                depth += 1
    52→                if depth > max_depth:
    53→                    max_depth = depth
    54→            for index in range(node.child_count - 1, -1, -1):
    55→                stack.append((node.children[index], depth))
    56→
    57→        if max_depth <= 1:
    58→            return None
    59→        return max_depth, f"callback depth {max_depth}"
    60→
    61→    return compute
    62→
```

I now have a thorough picture. Here is my assessment.

---

## Honest Assessment of the 16 Refactor Commits

### The Systemic Problem: Facade Proliferation

The dominant pattern across these commits is identical and mechanical: take a file, move its contents into new files with suffix-based names, and leave behind a **"Compatibility facade"** that re-exports everything. There are now **9 compatibility facade files** in `desloppify/languages/` alone. This is ironic given that `CLAUDE.md` explicitly says to **remove** re-export facades, and commit `303038d` does exactly that -- while the other 15 commits create new ones.

These facades are pure indirection. They exist so that existing importers don't need to change. But this is an internal tool with no backward-compatibility obligation (per your own policy). The facades should not exist -- importers should just be updated to point at the new modules.

### Per-Commit Breakdown

**1. `631054d` - split typescript patterns (347 -> 3 files + facade)**
Original was ~347 lines. Split into `patterns_analysis.py` (157), `patterns_catalog.py` (52), `patterns_cli.py` (99). The catalog at 52 lines is borderline tiny but it's a pure data declaration (pattern families), so that's a coherent unit. The analysis/CLI split is reasonable. **Verdict: Marginally justified, but the 52-line catalog file is gratuitous and the facade shouldn't exist.**

**2. `721aed4` - split typescript smell helpers (249 -> 2 files + facade)**
Original ~249 lines. Split into `_smell_helpers_blocks.py` (100) and `_smell_helpers_line_state.py` (108). These are "blocks" vs "line state" -- reasonable conceptual separation but the original was only 249 lines. **Verdict: Not justified. 249 lines is not large enough to warrant splitting.**

**3. `5e78423` - split typescript smell detectors (540 -> 3 files + facade)**
Original ~540 lines. Split into `_core` (166), `_flow` (230), `_safety` (141). The grouping (flow-control detectors vs safety detectors) makes conceptual sense and the original was genuinely large. **Verdict: Justified split, but the facade is unnecessary boilerplate.**

**4. `a53b18c` - split typescript commands (305 -> 1 file + smaller original)**
Original ~564 lines (305 removed). Split command wrappers (277 lines) from the registry. No facade created -- the commands.py imports from the new file. **Verdict: Reasonable. Clean separation, no facade.**

**5. `bf6d328` - reduce complexity in tree context detector**
Single-file refactor (136 lines, internal restructuring). No new files created. **Verdict: Good. This is what refactoring should look like.**

**6. `8e02f59` - split python AST node detectors (375 -> 3 files + facade)**
Original ~375 lines. Split into `_basic` (118), `_complexity` (103), `_nesting` (150). The grouping is coherent. **Verdict: Borderline. 375 lines is moderate, not monstrous. Acceptable if the facade is removed.**

**7. `3edc2d3` - split python mutable state (370 -> 2 files + facade)**
Original ~370 lines. Split into `mutable_state_ast.py` (198) and `mutable_state_scan.py` (130). AST-based vs scan-based detection is a real architectural boundary. **Verdict: Borderline justified. The 130-line scan file is small but coherent.**

**8. `23d6a48` - extract python dict-key visitor helpers (249 -> 1 file + smaller original)**
Original was ~500 lines (visitor.py). Extracted 240 lines of helper functions. No facade. **Verdict: Justified -- 500-line file with a natural seam.**

**9. `20d6f4e` - split python dict-key internals (331 -> 3 files + smaller __init__)**
Original __init__.py was ~331 lines. Split into `flow.py` (55), `schema.py` (175), `shared.py` (89). `flow.py` at 55 lines is too small to justify its own module. **Verdict: Over-split. flow.py is tiny.**

**10. `a22d604` - split csharp deps support (386 -> 3 files + facade)**
Original ~386 lines. Split into `metadata` (103), `projects` (170), `render` (113). Clear concern separation. **Verdict: Borderline justified, would be fine without the facade.**

**11. `b05a8fd` - split treesitter specs (771 -> 3 files + facade)**
Original was **771 lines** of pure data declarations (language specs). Split by language family: compiled (321), functional (122), scripting (242). **Verdict: Justified. 771 lines of repetitive config data benefits from grouping. But the facade (104 lines) that reassembles `TREESITTER_SPECS` is a lot of boilerplate.**

**12. `0638949` - split treesitter import resolvers (657 -> 3 files + facade)**
Original was **657 lines** with ~20 independent resolver functions. Split by language family: backend (189), functional (189), scripts (152). **Verdict: Justified. Each resolver is independent and the file was genuinely large.**

**13. `78320f2` - split treesitter complexity (363 -> 4 files + facade)**
Original was 363 lines. Split into FOUR files: `_shared` (44), `_callbacks` (61), `_nesting` (90), `_function_metrics` (180). `_shared` at **44 lines** and `_callbacks` at **61 lines** are clearly too small. **Verdict: Over-split. A 363-line file does not need to become 5 files. Two files (shared+nesting and function_metrics+callbacks) would have sufficed.**

**14. `18a8ec6` - extract lang runtime state accessors (121 lines extracted)**
Original runtime.py was 319 lines. Extracted ~90 lines of property accessors into a mixin class. The mixin pattern (LangRunStateAccessors) means LangRun now inherits from it. **Verdict: Questionable. Property accessor boilerplate was moved, not simplified. Now understanding LangRun requires reading two files. The inheritance adds conceptual overhead for no real benefit.**

**15. `58e0841` - split generic plugin assembly (363 -> 3 files + smaller original)**
Original was 363 lines. Split into `generic_capabilities` (98), `generic_registration` (142), `generic_structural` (179). No facade -- generic.py imports from the new modules. **Verdict: Borderline. The split axes (capabilities/registration/structural) are coherent but the original wasn't that large.**

**16. `303038d` - remove re-export facades and backward-compat aliases**
Merges `subjective_dimensions_core.py` back into `subjective_dimensions.py`, deletes a facade file, removes dead aliases. **Verdict: Excellent. This is the right kind of refactoring -- removing indirection. Ironic that it's undoing exactly the pattern the other 15 commits are creating.**

### Summary Statistics

| Metric | Count |
|--------|-------|
| New "compatibility facade" files created | 9 |
| New implementation files created | ~30 |
| Files under 60 lines | 4 (`_complexity_shared` 44, `flow.py` 55, `_complexity_callbacks` 61, `patterns_catalog` 52) |
| Original files that were genuinely too large (500+) | 4 (`_specs` 771, `_import_resolvers` 657, `_smell_detectors` 540, `visitor.py` 500) |
| Original files under 400 lines that were split | 6 |

### Verdict

**This is about 40% justified refactoring and 60% mechanical busywork.**

The good:
- Splitting 500-700+ line files (specs, import resolvers, smell detectors, visitor) is legitimate.
- Commit `303038d` (removing facades) and `bf6d328` (in-place simplification) are model refactors.
- The grouping axes (by language family, by concern) are generally coherent, not arbitrary.

The bad:
- **9 compatibility facades** are pure boilerplate that violates your own policy. These should not exist -- update the importers instead.
- Splitting 250-370 line files is premature. Those are normal-sized modules.
- Creating 44-line and 55-line files is over-splitting. These are not coherent units; they're fragments.
- The runtime mixin extraction (`LangRunStateAccessors`) adds inheritance complexity for zero simplification.
- The complexity split (363 lines -> 5 files) is the worst offender -- the `_complexity_shared.py` file is basically just one function and a type alias.

### Recommendations

1. **Delete all 9 compatibility facades.** Update importers to use the real module paths. This is what commit `303038d` already demonstrated is the right approach.
2. **Merge back the tiny files**: `_complexity_shared` + `_complexity_callbacks` into `_complexity_nesting`, and `flow.py` back into `dict_keys/shared.py`.
3. **Don't split files under ~400 lines** unless there's a compelling architectural reason (not just "separation of concerns" at a granularity that creates more files than functions).
4. The runtime accessor mixin should probably be inlined back -- property forwarding is inherently simple and doesn't benefit from being in a separate file.

```
1→"""Python facade detection helpers."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import ast
     6→from pathlib import Path
     7→
     8→from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import count_lines
     9→from desloppify.languages._framework.facade_common import (
    10→    facade_tier_confidence,
    11→    detect_reexport_facades_common,
    12→)
    13→
    14→
    15→def is_py_facade(filepath: str) -> dict | None:
    16→    """Check if a Python file is a pure re-export facade."""
    17→    try:
    18→        content = Path(filepath).read_text()
    19→        tree = ast.parse(content, filename=filepath)
    20→    except (OSError, SyntaxError, UnicodeDecodeError):
    21→        return None
    22→
    23→    if not tree.body:
    24→        return None
    25→
    26→    imports_from: list[str] = []
    27→    for node in tree.body:
    28→        if isinstance(node, ast.Import | ast.ImportFrom):
    29→            if isinstance(node, ast.ImportFrom) and node.module:
    30→                imports_from.append(node.module)
    31→            elif isinstance(node, ast.Import):
    32→                for alias in node.names:
    33→                    imports_from.append(alias.name)
    34→        elif isinstance(node, ast.Expr) and isinstance(
    35→            node.value, ast.Constant | ast.JoinedStr
    36→        ):
    37→            continue
    38→        elif isinstance(node, ast.Assign):
    39→            if (
    40→                len(node.targets) == 1
    41→                and isinstance(node.targets[0], ast.Name)
    42→                and node.targets[0].id == "__all__"
    43→            ):
    44→                continue
    45→            return None
    46→        else:
    47→            return None
    48→
    49→    if not imports_from:
    50→        return None
    51→
    52→    loc = len(content.splitlines())
    53→    return {"imports_from": imports_from, "loc": loc}
    54→
    55→
    56→def detect_reexport_facades(
    57→    graph: dict,
    58→) -> tuple[list[dict], int]:
    59→    """Detect Python re-export facade files and directories."""
    60→    entries, total_checked = detect_reexport_facades_common(
    61→        graph,
    62→        is_facade_fn=is_py_facade,
    63→    )
    64→
    65→    facade_files = {e["file"] for e in entries}
    66→    _detect_facade_directories(graph, facade_files, entries)
    67→    return sorted(
    68→        entries, key=lambda e: (e["kind"], e["importers"], -e["loc"])
    69→    ), total_checked
    70→
    71→
    72→def _detect_facade_directories(
    73→    graph: dict,
    74→    facade_files: set[str],
    75→    entries: list[dict],
    76→):
    77→    """Detect Python package directories where all modules are facades."""
    78→    by_dir: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
    79→    for filepath in graph:
    80→        parent = str(Path(filepath).parent)
    81→        by_dir.setdefault(parent, []).append(filepath)
    82→
    83→    for dirpath, files in by_dir.items():
    84→        init_file = str(Path(dirpath) / "__init__.py")
    85→        if init_file not in graph or init_file not in facade_files:
    86→            continue
    87→
    88→        non_init_files = [f for f in files if not f.endswith("__init__.py")]
    89→        if not non_init_files:
    90→            continue
    91→        if not all(f in facade_files for f in non_init_files):
    92→            continue
    93→
    94→        dir_importers = graph[init_file].get("importer_count", 0)
    95→        tier, confidence = facade_tier_confidence(dir_importers)
    96→        total_loc = sum(count_lines(Path(f)) for f in files if Path(f).exists())
    97→
    98→        entries.append(
    99→            {
   100→                "file": dirpath,
   101→                "loc": total_loc,
   102→                "importers": dir_importers,
   103→                "imports_from": [],
   104→                "kind": "directory",
   105→                "file_count": len(files),
   106→                "tier": tier,
   107→                "confidence": confidence,
   108→            }
   109→        )
   110→
```

```
1→"""TypeScript facade detection helpers."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import re
     6→from pathlib import Path
     7→
     8→from desloppify.languages._framework.facade_common import detect_reexport_facades_common
     9→
    10→
    11→def is_ts_facade(filepath: str) -> dict | None:
    12→    """Check if a TypeScript file is a pure re-export facade."""
    13→    try:
    14→        content = Path(filepath).read_text()
    15→        lines = content.splitlines()
    16→    except (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError):
    17→        return None
    18→
    19→    if not lines:
    20→        return None
    21→
    22→    imports_from: list[str] = []
    23→    export_re = re.compile(r"""^export\s+(?:\{[^}]*\}|\*)\s+from\s+['"]([^'"]+)['"]""")
    24→    reexport_re = re.compile(
    25→        r"""^export\s+(?:type\s+)?\{[^}]*\}\s+from\s+['"]([^'"]+)['"]"""
    26→    )
    27→
    28→    for line in lines:
    29→        stripped = line.strip()
    30→        if not stripped or stripped.startswith("//") or stripped.startswith("/*"):
    31→            continue
    32→
    33→        m = export_re.match(stripped) or reexport_re.match(stripped)
    34→        if m:
    35→            imports_from.append(m.group(1))
    36→            continue
    37→
    38→        return None
    39→
    40→    if not imports_from:
    41→        return None
    42→
    43→    return {"imports_from": imports_from, "loc": len(lines)}
    44→
    45→
    46→def detect_reexport_facades(
    47→    graph: dict,
    48→) -> tuple[list[dict], int]:
    49→    """Detect TypeScript re-export facade files."""
    50→    entries, total_checked = detect_reexport_facades_common(
    51→        graph,
    52→        is_facade_fn=is_ts_facade,
    53→    )
    54→
    55→    return sorted(
    56→        entries, key=lambda e: (e["kind"], e["importers"], -e["loc"])
    57→    ), total_checked
    58→
```

```
1→"""Shared helpers for language-specific re-export facade detectors."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from collections.abc import Callable
     6→
     7→DEFAULT_MAX_IMPORTERS = 20
     8→
     9→
    10→def facade_tier_confidence(importer_count: int) -> tuple[int, str]:
    11→    """Determine tier and confidence for a facade based on importer count.
    12→
    13→    ≤5 importers: tier 2, confidence high   (easy removal)
    14→    6-20 importers: tier 3, confidence medium (moderate effort)
    15→    >20 importers: tier 4, confidence medium  (large-scale refactor)
    16→    """
    17→    if importer_count <= 5:
    18→        return 2, "high"
    19→    if importer_count <= 20:
    20→        return 3, "medium"
    21→    return 4, "medium"
    22→
    23→
    24→def detect_reexport_facades_common(
    25→    graph: dict,
    26→    *,
    27→    is_facade_fn: Callable[[str], dict | None],
    28→    max_importers: int = DEFAULT_MAX_IMPORTERS,
    29→) -> tuple[list[dict], int]:
    30→    """Collect file-level re-export facades using a language detector callback.
    31→
    32→    By default this excludes very high-importer facades (importers > 20),
    33→    but callers can override the ceiling for broader scans.
    34→    """
    35→    entries: list[dict] = []
    36→    total_checked = 0
    37→
    38→    for filepath, node in graph.items():
    39→        total_checked += 1
    40→
    41→        result = is_facade_fn(filepath)
    42→        if not result:
    43→            continue
    44→
    45→        importer_count = node.get("importer_count", 0)
    46→        if importer_count > max_importers:
    47→            continue
    48→
    49→        tier, confidence = facade_tier_confidence(importer_count)
    50→
    51→        entries.append(
    52→            {
    53→                "file": filepath,
    54→                "loc": result["loc"],
    55→                "importers": importer_count,
    56→                "imports_from": result["imports_from"],
    57→                "kind": "file",
    58→                "tier": tier,
    59→                "confidence": confidence,
    60→            }
    61→        )
    62→
    63→    return entries, total_checked
    64→
    65→
    66→__all__ = [
    67→    "DEFAULT_MAX_IMPORTERS",
    68→    "facade_tier_confidence",
    69→    "detect_reexport_facades_common",
    70→]
    71→
```

```
1→"""Shared parser/bootstrap helpers for tree-sitter complexity signals."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import logging
     6→from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Any, Callable
     7→
     8→from . import PARSE_INIT_ERRORS
     9→from ._extractors import _get_parser
    10→
    11→if TYPE_CHECKING:
    12→    from desloppify.languages._framework.treesitter import TreeSitterLangSpec
    13→
    14→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    15→
    16→ComputeFn = Callable[[str, list[str]], tuple[int, str] | None]
    17→"""Signature for complexity signal compute functions.
    18→
    19→Each factory returns a closure with this effective call shape:
    20→``(content, lines, *, _filepath=\"\") -> (count, label) | None``.
    21→"""
    22→
    23→
    24→def _ensure_parser(
    25→    cache: dict[str, Any],
    26→    spec: TreeSitterLangSpec,
    27→    *,
    28→    with_query: bool = False,
    29→) -> bool:
    30→    """Lazily initialise parser (and optionally function query) into *cache*."""
    31→    if "parser" in cache:
    32→        return True
    33→    try:
    34→        parser, lang = _get_parser(spec.grammar)
    35→        cache["parser"] = parser
    36→        cache["language"] = lang
    37→        if with_query:
    38→            from ._extractors import _make_query
    39→
    40→            cache["query"] = _make_query(lang, spec.function_query)
    41→    except PARSE_INIT_ERRORS as exc:
    42→        logger.debug("tree-sitter init failed: %s", exc)
    43→        return False
    44→    return True
    45→
```

```
1→"""Callback/closure nesting metric for tree-sitter complexity."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Any
     6→
     7→from ._cache import _PARSE_CACHE
     8→from ._complexity_shared import ComputeFn, _ensure_parser
     9→
    10→if TYPE_CHECKING:
    11→    from desloppify.languages._framework.treesitter import TreeSitterLangSpec
    12→
    13→
    14→_CLOSURE_NODE_TYPES = frozenset(
    15→    {
    16→        "arrow_function",
    17→        "function_expression",
    18→        "function",
    19→        "lambda_expression",
    20→        "closure_expression",
    21→        "lambda",
    22→        "anonymous_function",
    23→        "block_argument",
    24→        "func_literal",
    25→    }
    26→)
    27→
    28→
    29→def make_callback_depth_compute(spec: TreeSitterLangSpec) -> ComputeFn:
    30→    """Build a complexity compute callback for callback/closure nesting depth."""
    31→    _cached_parser: dict[str, Any] = {}
    32→
    33→    def compute(content: str, lines: list[str], *, _filepath: str = "") -> tuple[int, str] | None:
    34→        del content, lines
    35→        if not _filepath:
    36→            return None
    37→        if not _ensure_parser(_cached_parser, spec):
    38→            return None
    39→
    40→        parser = _cached_parser["parser"]
    41→        cached = _PARSE_CACHE.get_or_parse(_filepath, parser, spec.grammar)
    42→        if cached is None:
    43→            return None
    44→        _source, tree = cached
    45→
    46→        max_depth = 0
    47→        stack: list[tuple[object, int]] = [(tree.root_node, 0)]
    48→        while stack:
    49→            node, depth = stack.pop()
    50→            if node.type in _CLOSURE_NODE_TYPES:
    51→                depth += 1
    52→                if depth > max_depth:
    53→                    max_depth = depth
    54→            for index in range(node.child_count - 1, -1, -1):
    55→                stack.append((node.children[index], depth))
    56→
    57→        if max_depth <= 1:
    58→            return None
    59→        return max_depth, f"callback depth {max_depth}"
    60→
    61→    return compute
    62→
```

```
1→"""Single-scope dict key flow analysis for Python source."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import ast
     6→import importlib
     7→import logging
     8→from pathlib import Path
     9→
    10→from desloppify.base.discovery.paths import get_project_root
    11→from desloppify.base.discovery.source import find_py_files
    12→
    13→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    14→
    15→
    16→def _load_dict_key_visitor():
    17→    module = importlib.import_module(".visitor", package=__package__)
    18→    return module.DictKeyVisitor
    19→
    20→
    21→def detect_dict_key_flow(path: Path) -> tuple[list[dict], int]:
    22→    """Walk all .py files and run DictKeyVisitor. Returns (issues, files_checked)."""
    23→    dict_key_visitor = _load_dict_key_visitor()
    24→    files = find_py_files(path)
    25→    all_issues: list[dict] = []
    26→
    27→    for filepath in files:
    28→        try:
    29→            file_path = (
    30→                Path(filepath) if Path(filepath).is_absolute() else get_project_root() / filepath
    31→            )
    32→            source = file_path.read_text()
    33→        except (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError) as exc:
    34→            logger.debug(
    35→                "Skipping unreadable python file %s in dict-key pass: %s",
    36→                filepath,
    37→                exc,
    38→            )
    39→            continue
    40→
    41→        try:
    42→            tree = ast.parse(source, filename=filepath)
    43→        except SyntaxError as exc:
    44→            logger.debug(
    45→                "Skipping unparseable python file %s in dict-key pass: %s",
    46→                filepath,
    47→                exc,
    48→            )
    49→            continue
    50→
    51→        visitor = dict_key_visitor(filepath)
    52→        visitor.visit(tree)
    53→        all_issues.extend(visitor._issues)
    54→
    55→    return all_issues, len(files)
    56→
```

```
1→"""Compatibility facade for TypeScript smell helper utilities."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from typing import NamedTuple
     6→
     7→from desloppify.base.text_utils import strip_c_style_comments
     8→from desloppify.languages.typescript.detectors._smell_helpers_blocks import (
     9→    _code_text,
    10→    _content_line_info,
    11→    _extract_block_body,
    12→    _find_block_end,
    13→    _track_brace_body,
    14→)
    15→from desloppify.languages.typescript.detectors._smell_helpers_line_state import (
    16→    _build_ts_line_state,
    17→    _scan_template_content,
    18→)
    19→from desloppify.languages.typescript.syntax.scanner import scan_code
    20→
    21→__all__ = [
    22→    "_FileContext",
    23→    "_build_ts_line_state",
    24→    "_code_text",
    25→    "_content_line_info",
    26→    "_extract_block_body",
    27→    "_find_block_end",
    28→    "_scan_template_content",
    29→    "_strip_ts_comments",
    30→    "_track_brace_body",
    31→    "_ts_match_is_in_string",
    32→    "scan_code",
    33→]
    34→
    35→
    36→class _FileContext(NamedTuple):
    37→    """Per-file data bundle passed to all smell detectors."""
    38→
    39→    filepath: str
    40→    content: str
    41→    lines: list[str]
    42→    line_state: dict[int, str]
    43→
    44→
    45→def _strip_ts_comments(text: str) -> str:
    46→    """Strip // and /* */ comments while preserving strings."""
    47→    return strip_c_style_comments(text)
    48→
    49→
    50→def _ts_match_is_in_string(line: str, match_start: int) -> bool:
    51→    """Check if a match position falls inside a string literal/comment on one line."""
    52→    i = 0
    53→    in_str = None
    54→
    55→    while i < len(line):
    56→        if i == match_start:
    57→            return in_str is not None
    58→
    59→        ch = line[i]
    60→
    61→        if in_str and ch == "\\" and i + 1 < len(line):
    62→            i += 2
    63→            continue
    64→
    65→        if in_str:
    66→            if ch == in_str:
    67→                in_str = None
    68→            i += 1
    69→            continue
    70→
    71→        if ch == "/" and i + 1 < len(line) and line[i + 1] == "/":
    72→            return match_start > i
    73→
    74→        if ch in ("'", '"', "`"):
    75→            in_str = ch
    76→            i += 1
    77→            continue
    78→
    79→        i += 1
    80→
    81→    return False
    82→
```

```
    30→    "_track_brace_body",
    31→    "_ts_match_is_in_string",
    32→    "scan_code",
    33→]
    34→
    35→
    36→class _FileContext(NamedTuple):
    37→    """Per-file data bundle passed to all smell detectors."""
    38→
    39→    filepath: str
    40→    content: str
    41→    lines: list[str]
    42→    line_state: dict[int, str]
    43→
    44→
    45→def _strip_ts_comments(text: str) -> str:
    46→    """Strip // and /* */ comments while preserving strings."""
    47→    return strip_c_style_comments(text)
    48→
    49→
    50→def _ts_match_is_in_string(line: str, match_start: int) -> bool:
    51→    """Check if a match position falls inside a string literal/comment on one line."""
    52→    i = 0
    53→    in_str = None
    54→
    55→    while i < len(line):
    56→        if i == match_start:
    57→            return in_str is not None
    58→
    59→        ch = line[i]
    60→
    61→        if in_str and ch == "\\" and i + 1 < len(line):
    62→            i += 2
    63→            continue
    64→
    65→        if in_str:
    66→            if ch == in_str:
    67→                in_str = None
    68→            i += 1
    69→            continue
    70→
    71→        if ch == "/" and i + 1 < len(line) and line[i + 1] == "/":
    72→            return match_start > i
    73→
    74→        if ch in ("'", '"', "`"):
    75→            in_str = ch
    76→            i += 1
    77→            continue
    78→
    79→        i += 1
    80→
    81→    return False
    82→
```

```
1→"""Shared parser/bootstrap helpers for tree-sitter complexity signals."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import logging
     6→from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Any, Callable
     7→
     8→from . import PARSE_INIT_ERRORS
     9→from ._extractors import _get_parser
    10→
    11→if TYPE_CHECKING:
    12→    from desloppify.languages._framework.treesitter import TreeSitterLangSpec
    13→
    14→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    15→
    16→ComputeFn = Callable[[str, list[str]], tuple[int, str] | None]
    17→"""Signature for complexity signal compute functions.
    18→
    19→Each factory returns a closure with this effective call shape:
    20→``(content, lines, *, _filepath=\"\") -> (count, label) | None``.
    21→"""
    22→
    23→
    24→def _ensure_parser(
    25→    cache: dict[str, Any],
    26→    spec: TreeSitterLangSpec,
    27→    *,
    28→    with_query: bool = False,
    29→) -> bool:
    30→    """Lazily initialise parser (and optionally function query) into *cache*."""
    31→    if "parser" in cache:
    32→        return True
    33→    try:
    34→        parser, lang = _get_parser(spec.grammar)
    35→        cache["parser"] = parser
    36→        cache["language"] = lang
    37→        if with_query:
    38→            from ._extractors import _make_query
    39→
    40→            cache["query"] = _make_query(lang, spec.function_query)
    41→    except PARSE_INIT_ERRORS as exc:
    42→        logger.debug("tree-sitter init failed: %s", exc)
    43→        return False
    44→    return True
    45→
```

```
1→"""Callback/closure nesting metric for tree-sitter complexity."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Any
     6→
     7→from ._cache import _PARSE_CACHE
     8→from ._complexity_shared import ComputeFn, _ensure_parser
     9→
    10→if TYPE_CHECKING:
    11→    from desloppify.languages._framework.treesitter import TreeSitterLangSpec
    12→
    13→
    14→_CLOSURE_NODE_TYPES = frozenset(
    15→    {
    16→        "arrow_function",
    17→        "function_expression",
    18→        "function",
    19→        "lambda_expression",
    20→        "closure_expression",
    21→        "lambda",
    22→        "anonymous_function",
    23→        "block_argument",
    24→        "func_literal",
    25→    }
    26→)
    27→
    28→
    29→def make_callback_depth_compute(spec: TreeSitterLangSpec) -> ComputeFn:
    30→    """Build a complexity compute callback for callback/closure nesting depth."""
    31→    _cached_parser: dict[str, Any] = {}
    32→
    33→    def compute(content: str, lines: list[str], *, _filepath: str = "") -> tuple[int, str] | None:
    34→        del content, lines
    35→        if not _filepath:
    36→            return None
    37→        if not _ensure_parser(_cached_parser, spec):
    38→            return None
    39→
    40→        parser = _cached_parser["parser"]
    41→        cached = _PARSE_CACHE.get_or_parse(_filepath, parser, spec.grammar)
    42→        if cached is None:
    43→            return None
    44→        _source, tree = cached
    45→
    46→        max_depth = 0
    47→        stack: list[tuple[object, int]] = [(tree.root_node, 0)]
    48→        while stack:
    49→            node, depth = stack.pop()
    50→            if node.type in _CLOSURE_NODE_TYPES:
    51→                depth += 1
    52→                if depth > max_depth:
    53→                    max_depth = depth
    54→            for index in range(node.child_count - 1, -1, -1):
    55→                stack.append((node.children[index], depth))
    56→
    57→        if max_depth <= 1:
    58→            return None
    59→        return max_depth, f"callback depth {max_depth}"
    60→
    61→    return compute
    62→
```

```
1→"""Nesting-depth complexity metrics for tree-sitter languages."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Any
     6→
     7→from ._cache import _PARSE_CACHE
     8→from ._complexity_shared import ComputeFn, _ensure_parser
     9→
    10→if TYPE_CHECKING:
    11→    from desloppify.languages._framework.treesitter import TreeSitterLangSpec
    12→
    13→
    14→_NESTING_NODE_TYPES = frozenset(
    15→    {
    16→        "if_statement",
    17→        "if_expression",
    18→        "if_let_expression",
    19→        "else_clause",
    20→        "elif_clause",
    21→        "for_statement",
    22→        "for_expression",
    23→        "for_in_statement",
    24→        "while_statement",
    25→        "while_expression",
    26→        "do_statement",
    27→        "loop_expression",
    28→        "try_statement",
    29→        "try_expression",
    30→        "catch_clause",
    31→        "rescue",
    32→        "except_clause",
    33→        "switch_statement",
    34→        "switch_expression",
    35→        "match_expression",
    36→        "case_clause",
    37→        "match_arm",
    38→        "with_statement",
    39→        "with_clause",
    40→        "lambda_expression",
    41→        "closure_expression",
    42→    }
    43→)
    44→
    45→
    46→def compute_nesting_depth_ts(
    47→    filepath: str, spec: TreeSitterLangSpec, parser, language
    48→) -> int | None:
    49→    """Compute max control-flow nesting depth via iterative AST walk."""
    50→    del language
    51→    cached = _PARSE_CACHE.get_or_parse(filepath, parser, spec.grammar)
    52→    if cached is None:
    53→        return None
    54→    _source, tree = cached
    55→
    56→    max_depth = 0
    57→    stack: list[tuple[object, int]] = [(tree.root_node, 0)]
    58→    while stack:
    59→        node, depth = stack.pop()
    60→        if node.type in _NESTING_NODE_TYPES:
    61→            depth += 1
    62→            if depth > max_depth:
    63→                max_depth = depth
    64→        for index in range(node.child_count - 1, -1, -1):
    65→            stack.append((node.children[index], depth))
    66→    return max_depth
    67→
    68→
    69→def make_nesting_depth_compute(spec: TreeSitterLangSpec) -> ComputeFn:
    70→    """Build a complexity compute callback for max nesting depth."""
    71→    _cached_parser: dict[str, Any] = {}
    72→
    73→    def compute(content: str, lines: list[str], *, _filepath: str = "") -> tuple[int, str] | None:
    74→        del content, lines
    75→        if not _filepath:
    76→            return None
    77→        if not _ensure_parser(_cached_parser, spec):
    78→            return None
    79→
    80→        depth = compute_nesting_depth_ts(
    81→            _filepath,
    82→            spec,
    83→            _cached_parser["parser"],
    84→            _cached_parser["language"],
    85→        )
    86→        if depth is None or depth <= 0:
    87→            return None
    88→        return depth, f"nesting depth {depth}"
    89→
    90→    return compute
    91→
```

```
1→"""Function-oriented complexity metrics (length, cyclomatic, params)."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Any
     6→
     7→from ._cache import _PARSE_CACHE
     8→from ._complexity_shared import ComputeFn, _ensure_parser
     9→
    10→if TYPE_CHECKING:
    11→    from desloppify.languages._framework.treesitter import TreeSitterLangSpec
    12→
    13→
    14→def make_long_functions_compute(spec: TreeSitterLangSpec) -> ComputeFn:
    15→    """Build a complexity compute callback for longest function LOC."""
    16→    from ._extractors import _run_query, _unwrap_node
    17→
    18→    _cached_parser: dict[str, Any] = {}
    19→
    20→    def compute(content: str, lines: list[str], *, _filepath: str = "") -> tuple[int, str] | None:
    21→        del content, lines
    22→        if not _filepath:
    23→            return None
    24→        if not _ensure_parser(_cached_parser, spec, with_query=True):
    25→            return None
    26→
    27→        parser = _cached_parser["parser"]
    28→        query = _cached_parser["query"]
    29→        cached = _PARSE_CACHE.get_or_parse(_filepath, parser, spec.grammar)
    30→        if cached is None:
    31→            return None
    32→        _source, tree = cached
    33→
    34→        matches = _run_query(query, tree.root_node)
    35→        max_loc = 0
    36→        for _pattern_idx, captures in matches:
    37→            func_node = _unwrap_node(captures.get("func"))
    38→            if not func_node:
    39→                continue
    40→            loc = func_node.end_point[0] - func_node.start_point[0] + 1
    41→            if loc > max_loc:
    42→                max_loc = loc
    43→
    44→        if max_loc <= 0:
    45→            return None
    46→        return max_loc, f"longest function {max_loc} LOC"
    47→
    48→    return compute
    49→
    50→
    51→_BRANCHING_NODE_TYPES = frozenset(
    52→    {
    53→        "if_statement",
    54→        "if_expression",
    55→        "if_let_expression",
    56→        "elif_clause",
    57→        "else_if",
    58→        "for_statement",
    59→        "for_expression",
    60→        "for_in_statement",
    61→        "while_statement",
    62→        "while_expression",
    63→        "do_statement",
    64→        "loop_expression",
    65→        "case_clause",
    66→        "match_arm",
    67→        "catch_clause",
    68→        "rescue",
    69→        "except_clause",
    70→        "ternary_expression",
    71→        "conditional_expression",
    72→        "binary_expression",
    73→    }
    74→)
    75→
    76→_LOGICAL_OPS = frozenset({"&&", "||", "and", "or"})
    77→
    78→
    79→def _count_decisions(node) -> int:
    80→    """Count decision points in a function subtree."""
    81→    count = 0
    82→    stack = [node]
    83→    while stack:
    84→        current = stack.pop()
    85→        if current.type in _BRANCHING_NODE_TYPES:
    86→            if current.type == "binary_expression":
    87→                for child in current.children:
    88→                    child_text = child.text if isinstance(child.text, str) else child.text
    89→                    if child.type in _LOGICAL_OPS or (
    90→                        child.child_count == 0
    91→                        and child_text
    92→                        and (
    93→                            child_text
    94→                            if isinstance(child_text, str)
    95→                            else child_text.decode("utf-8", "replace")
    96→                        )
    97→                        in _LOGICAL_OPS
    98→                    ):
    99→                        count += 1
   100→                        break
   101→            else:
   102→                count += 1
   103→        for index in range(current.child_count - 1, -1, -1):
   104→            stack.append(current.children[index])
   105→    return count
   106→
   107→
   108→def make_cyclomatic_complexity_compute(spec: TreeSitterLangSpec) -> ComputeFn:
   109→    """Build a complexity compute callback for max per-function cyclomatic score."""
   110→    from ._extractors import _run_query, _unwrap_node
   111→
   112→    _cached_parser: dict[str, Any] = {}
   113→
   114→    def compute(content: str, lines: list[str], *, _filepath: str = "") -> tuple[int, str] | None:
   115→        del content, lines
   116→        if not _filepath:
   117→            return None
   118→        if not _ensure_parser(_cached_parser, spec, with_query=True):
   119→            return None
   120→
   121→        parser = _cached_parser["parser"]
   122→        query = _cached_parser["query"]
   123→        cached = _PARSE_CACHE.get_or_parse(_filepath, parser, spec.grammar)
   124→        if cached is None:
   125→            return None
   126→        _source, tree = cached
   127→
   128→        matches = _run_query(query, tree.root_node)
   129→        max_cc = 0
   130→        for _pattern_idx, captures in matches:
   131→            func_node = _unwrap_node(captures.get("func"))
   132→            if not func_node:
   133→                continue
   134→            cc = 1 + _count_decisions(func_node)
   135→            if cc > max_cc:
   136→                max_cc = cc
   137→
   138→        if max_cc <= 1:
   139→            return None
   140→        return max_cc, f"cyclomatic complexity {max_cc}"
   141→
   142→    return compute
   143→
   144→
   145→def make_max_params_compute(spec: TreeSitterLangSpec) -> ComputeFn:
   146→    """Build a complexity compute callback for max parameter count."""
   147→    from ._extractors import _extract_param_names, _run_query, _unwrap_node
   148→
   149→    _cached_parser: dict[str, Any] = {}
   150→
   151→    def compute(content: str, lines: list[str], *, _filepath: str = "") -> tuple[int, str] | None:
   152→        del content, lines
   153→        if not _filepath:
   154→            return None
   155→        if not _ensure_parser(_cached_parser, spec, with_query=True):
   156→            return None
   157→
   158→        parser = _cached_parser["parser"]
   159→        query = _cached_parser["query"]
   160→        cached = _PARSE_CACHE.get_or_parse(_filepath, parser, spec.grammar)
   161→        if cached is None:
   162→            return None
   163→        _source, tree = cached
   164→
   165→        matches = _run_query(query, tree.root_node)
   166→        max_params = 0
   167→        for _pattern_idx, captures in matches:
   168→            func_node = _unwrap_node(captures.get("func"))
   169→            if not func_node:
   170→                continue
   171→            params = _extract_param_names(func_node)
   172→            params = [param for param in params if param not in ("self", "cls", "this")]
   173→            if len(params) > max_params:
   174→                max_params = len(params)
   175→
   176→        if max_params <= 0:
   177→            return None
   178→        return max_params, f"{max_params} params"
   179→
   180→    return compute
   181→
```

```
1→"""Compatibility facade for TypeScript smell helper utilities."""

     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from typing import NamedTuple
     6→
     7→from desloppify.base.text_utils import strip_c_style_comments
     8→from desloppify.languages.typescript.detectors._smell_helpers_blocks import (
     9→    _code_text,
    10→    _content_line_info,
    11→    _extract_block_body,
    12→    _find_block_end,
    13→    _track_brace_body,
    14→)
    15→from desloppify.languages.typescript.detectors._smell_helpers_line_state import (
    16→    _build_ts_line_state,
    17→    _scan_template_content,
    18→)
    19→from desloppify.languages.typescript.syntax.scanner import scan_code
    20→
    21→__all__ = [
    22→    "_FileContext",
    23→    "_build_ts_line_state",
    24→    "_code_text",
    25→    "_content_line_info",
    26→    "_extract_block_body",
    27→    "_find_block_end",
    28→    "_scan_template_content",
    29→    "_strip_ts_comments",
    30→    "_track_brace_body",
    31→    "_ts_match_is_in_string",
    32→    "scan_code",
    33→]
    34→
    35→
    36→class _FileContext(NamedTuple):
    37→    """Per-file data bundle passed to all smell detectors."""
    38→
    39→    filepath: str
    40→    content: str
    41→    lines: list[str]
    42→    line_state: dict[int, str]
    43→
    44→
    45→def _strip_ts_comments(text: str) -> str:
    46→    """Strip // and /* */ comments while preserving strings."""
    47→    return strip_c_style_comments(text)
    48→
    49→
    50→def _ts_match_is_in_string(line: str, match_start: int) -> bool:
    51→    """Check if a match position falls inside a string literal/comment on one line."""
    52→    i = 0
    53→    in_str = None
    54→
    55→    while i < len(line):
    56→        if i == match_start:
    57→            return in_str is not None
    58→
    59→        ch = line[i]
    60→
    61→        if in_str and ch == "\\" and i + 1 < len(line):
    62→            i += 2
    63→            continue
    64→
    65→        if in_str:
    66→            if ch == in_str:
    67→                in_str = None
    68→            i += 1
    69→            continue
    70→
    71→        if ch == "/" and i + 1 < len(line) and line[i + 1] == "/":
    72→            return match_start > i
    73→
    74→        if ch in ("'", '"', "`"):
    75→            in_str = ch
    76→            i += 1
    77→            continue
    78→
    79→        i += 1
    80→
    81→    return False
    82→
```

```
1→"""Block parsing and code-text projection helpers for TS smell detectors."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.languages.typescript.syntax.scanner import scan_code
     6→
     7→
     8→def _track_brace_body(
     9→    lines: list[str], start_line: int, *, max_scan: int = 2000
    10→) -> int | None:
    11→    """Find the closing brace matching the first opening brace from start_line."""
    12→    depth = 0
    13→    found_open = False
    14→    for line_idx in range(start_line, min(start_line + max_scan, len(lines))):
    15→        for _, ch, in_string in scan_code(lines[line_idx]):
    16→            if in_string:
    17→                continue
    18→            if ch == "{":
    19→                depth += 1
    20→                found_open = True
    21→            elif ch == "}":
    22→                depth -= 1
    23→                if found_open and depth == 0:
    24→                    return line_idx
    25→    return None
    26→
    27→
    28→def _find_block_end(content: str, brace_start: int, max_scan: int = 5000) -> int | None:
    29→    """Find the closing brace position in a content string from an opening brace."""
    30→    depth = 0
    31→    for ci, ch, in_s in scan_code(
    32→        content, brace_start, min(brace_start + max_scan, len(content))
    33→    ):
    34→        if in_s:
    35→            continue
    36→        if ch == "{":
    37→            depth += 1
    38→        elif ch == "}":
    39→            depth -= 1
    40→            if depth == 0:
    41→                return ci
    42→    return None
    43→
    44→
    45→def _extract_block_body(
    46→    content: str, brace_start: int, max_scan: int = 5000
    47→) -> str | None:
    48→    """Return text between ``{`` at brace_start and its matching ``}``."""
    49→    end = _find_block_end(content, brace_start, max_scan)
    50→    if end is None:
    51→        return None
    52→    return content[brace_start + 1 : end]
    53→
    54→
    55→def _content_line_info(content: str, pos: int) -> tuple[int, str]:
    56→    """Return ``(line_no, stripped snippet[:100])`` for a position in content."""
    57→    line_no = content[:pos].count("\n") + 1
    58→    line_start = content.rfind("\n", 0, pos) + 1
    59→    line_end = content.find("\n", pos)
    60→    if line_end == -1:
    61→        line_end = len(content)
    62→    return line_no, content[line_start:line_end].strip()[:100]
    63→
    64→
    65→def _code_text(text: str) -> str:
    66→    """Blank string literals and ``//`` comments to spaces, preserving positions."""
    67→    out = list(text)
    68→    in_line_comment = False
    69→    prev_code_idx = -2
    70→    prev_code_ch = ""
    71→    for i, ch, in_s in scan_code(text):
    72→        if ch == "\n":
    73→            in_line_comment = False
    74→            prev_code_ch = ""
    75→            continue
    76→        if in_line_comment:
    77→            out[i] = " "
    78→            continue
    79→        if in_s:
    80→            out[i] = " "
    81→            continue
    82→        if ch == "/" and prev_code_ch == "/" and prev_code_idx == i - 1:
    83→            out[prev_code_idx] = " "
    84→            out[i] = " "
    85→            in_line_comment = True
    86→            prev_code_ch = ""
    87→            continue
    88→        prev_code_idx = i
    89→        prev_code_ch = ch
    90→    return "".join(out)
    91→
    92→
    93→__all__ = [
    94→    "_code_text",
    95→    "_content_line_info",
    96→    "_extract_block_body",
    97→    "_find_block_end",
    98→    "_track_brace_body",
    99→    "scan_code",
   100→]
   101→
```

```
1→"""Line-state scanners for block comments and template literals."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→
     6→def _scan_template_content(
     7→    line: str, start: int, brace_depth: int = 0
     8→) -> tuple[int, bool, int]:
     9→    """Scan template literal content from *start* in *line*."""
    10→    j = start
    11→    while j < len(line):
    12→        ch = line[j]
    13→        if ch == "\\" and j + 1 < len(line):
    14→            j += 2
    15→            continue
    16→        if ch == "$" and j + 1 < len(line) and line[j + 1] == "{":
    17→            brace_depth += 1
    18→            j += 2
    19→            continue
    20→        if ch == "}" and brace_depth > 0:
    21→            brace_depth -= 1
    22→            j += 1
    23→            continue
    24→        if ch == "`" and brace_depth == 0:
    25→            return (j + 1, True, brace_depth)
    26→        j += 1
    27→    return (j, False, brace_depth)
    28→
    29→
    30→def _scan_code_line(line: str) -> tuple[bool, bool, int]:
    31→    """Scan a normal code line for block comment or template literal start."""
    32→    j = 0
    33→    in_str = None
    34→    while j < len(line):
    35→        ch = line[j]
    36→
    37→        if in_str and ch == "\\" and j + 1 < len(line):
    38→            j += 2
    39→            continue
    40→
    41→        if in_str:
    42→            if ch == in_str:
    43→                in_str = None
    44→            j += 1
    45→            continue
    46→
    47→        if ch == "/" and j + 1 < len(line) and line[j + 1] == "/":
    48→            break
    49→
    50→        if ch == "/" and j + 1 < len(line) and line[j + 1] == "*":
    51→            close = line.find("*/", j + 2)
    52→            if close != -1:
    53→                j = close + 2
    54→                continue
    55→            return (True, False, 0)
    56→
    57→        if ch == "`":
    58→            end_pos, found_close, depth = _scan_template_content(line, j + 1)
    59→            if found_close:
    60→                j = end_pos
    61→                continue
    62→            return (False, True, depth)
    63→
    64→        if ch in ("'", '"'):
    65→            in_str = ch
    66→            j += 1
    67→            continue
    68→
    69→        j += 1
    70→
    71→    return (False, False, 0)
    72→
    73→
    74→def _build_ts_line_state(lines: list[str]) -> dict[int, str]:
    75→    """Build a map of lines inside block comments or template literals."""
    76→    state: dict[int, str] = {}
    77→    in_block_comment = False
    78→    in_template = False
    79→    template_brace_depth = 0
    80→
    81→    for i, line in enumerate(lines):
    82→        if in_block_comment:
    83→            state[i] = "block_comment"
    84→            if "*/" in line:
    85→                in_block_comment = False
    86→            continue
    87→
    88→        if in_template:
    89→            state[i] = "template_literal"
    90→            _, found_close, template_brace_depth = _scan_template_content(
    91→                line, 0, template_brace_depth
    92→            )
    93→            if found_close:
    94→                in_template = False
    95→            continue
    96→
    97→        in_block_comment, in_template, depth = _scan_code_line(line)
    98→        if in_template:
    99→            template_brace_depth = depth
   100→
   101→    return state
   102→
   103→
   104→__all__ = [
   105→    "_build_ts_line_state",
   106→    "_scan_code_line",
   107→    "_scan_template_content",
   108→]
   109→
```

```
1→"""Single-scope dict key flow analysis for Python source."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import ast
     6→import importlib
     7→import logging
     8→from pathlib import Path
     9→
    10→from desloppify.base.discovery.paths import get_project_root
    11→from desloppify.base.discovery.source import find_py_files
    12→
    13→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    14→
    15→
    16→def _load_dict_key_visitor():
    17→    module = importlib.import_module(".visitor", package=__package__)
    18→    return module.DictKeyVisitor
    19→
    20→
    21→def detect_dict_key_flow(path: Path) -> tuple[list[dict], int]:
    22→    """Walk all .py files and run DictKeyVisitor. Returns (issues, files_checked)."""
    23→    dict_key_visitor = _load_dict_key_visitor()
    24→    files = find_py_files(path)
    25→    all_issues: list[dict] = []
    26→
    27→    for filepath in files:
    28→        try:
    29→            file_path = (
    30→                Path(filepath) if Path(filepath).is_absolute() else get_project_root() / filepath
    31→            )
    32→            source = file_path.read_text()
    33→        except (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError) as exc:
    34→            logger.debug(
    35→                "Skipping unreadable python file %s in dict-key pass: %s",
    36→                filepath,
    37→                exc,
    38→            )
    39→            continue
    40→
    41→        try:
    42→            tree = ast.parse(source, filename=filepath)
    43→        except SyntaxError as exc:
    44→            logger.debug(
    45→                "Skipping unparseable python file %s in dict-key pass: %s",
    46→                filepath,
    47→                exc,
    48→            )
    49→            continue
    50→
    51→        visitor = dict_key_visitor(filepath)
    52→        visitor.visit(tree)
    53→        all_issues.extend(visitor._issues)
    54→
    55→    return all_issues, len(files)
    56→
```

```
1→"""Shared primitives for Python dict-key detector components."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import ast
     6→from collections import defaultdict
     7→from dataclasses import dataclass, field
     8→
     9→
    10→@dataclass
    11→class TrackedDict:
    12→    """A dict variable tracked within a single scope."""
    13→
    14→    name: str
    15→    created_line: int
    16→    locally_created: bool
    17→    returned_or_passed: bool = False
    18→    has_dynamic_key: bool = False
    19→    has_star_unpack: bool = False
    20→    writes: dict[str, list[int]] = field(default_factory=lambda: defaultdict(list))
    21→    reads: dict[str, list[int]] = field(default_factory=lambda: defaultdict(list))
    22→    bulk_read: bool = False
    23→
    24→
    25→_CONFIG_NAMES = {
    26→    "config",
    27→    "settings",
    28→    "defaults",
    29→    "options",
    30→    "kwargs",
    31→    "context",
    32→    "ctx",
    33→    "env",
    34→    "params",
    35→    "metadata",
    36→    "headers",
    37→    "attrs",
    38→    "attributes",
    39→    "props",
    40→    "properties",
    41→}
    42→
    43→_READ_METHODS = {"get", "pop", "setdefault", "__getitem__", "__contains__"}
    44→_WRITE_METHODS = {"update", "setdefault", "__setitem__"}
    45→_BULK_READ_METHODS = {"keys", "values", "items", "copy", "__iter__"}
    46→
    47→
    48→def _levenshtein(s1: str, s2: str) -> int:
    49→    """Compute Levenshtein edit distance between two strings."""
    50→    if len(s1) < len(s2):
    51→        return _levenshtein(s2, s1)
    52→    if len(s2) == 0:
    53→        return len(s1)
    54→    prev = list(range(len(s2) + 1))
    55→    for i, c1 in enumerate(s1):
    56→        curr = [i + 1]
    57→        for j, c2 in enumerate(s2):
    58→            cost = 0 if c1 == c2 else 1
    59→            curr.append(min(curr[j] + 1, prev[j + 1] + 1, prev[j] + cost))
    60→        prev = curr
    61→    return prev[-1]
    62→
    63→
    64→def _is_singular_plural(a: str, b: str) -> bool:
    65→    """Check if a and b are singular/plural variants of each other."""
    66→    return (
    67→        a + "s" == b
    68→        or b + "s" == a
    69→        or a + "es" == b
    70→        or b + "es" == a
    71→        or (a.endswith("ies") and a[:-3] + "y" == b)
    72→        or (b.endswith("ies") and b[:-3] + "y" == a)
    73→    )
    74→
    75→
    76→def _get_name(node: ast.expr) -> str | None:
    77→    """Extract variable name from a Name or Attribute(self.x) node."""
    78→    if isinstance(node, ast.Name):
    79→        return node.id
    80→    if isinstance(node, ast.Attribute) and isinstance(node.value, ast.Name):
    81→        return f"{node.value.id}.{node.attr}"
    82→    return None
    83→
    84→
    85→def _get_str_key(node: ast.expr) -> str | None:
    86→    """Extract a string literal from a subscript slice."""
    87→    if isinstance(node, ast.Constant) and isinstance(node.value, str):
    88→        return node.value
    89→    return None
    90→
```

```
1→"""Nesting-depth complexity metrics for tree-sitter languages."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Any
     6→
     7→from ._cache import _PARSE_CACHE
     8→from ._complexity_shared import ComputeFn, _ensure_parser
     9→
    10→if TYPE_CHECKING:
    11→    from desloppify.languages._framework.treesitter import TreeSitterLangSpec
    12→
    13→
    14→_NESTING_NODE_TYPES = frozenset(
    15→    {
    16→        "if_statement",
    17→        "if_expression",
    18→        "if_let_expression",
    19→        "else_clause",
    20→        "elif_clause",
    21→        "for_statement",
    22→        "for_expression",
    23→        "for_in_statement",
    24→        "while_statement",
    25→        "while_expression",
    26→        "do_statement",
    27→        "loop_expression",
    28→        "try_statement",
    29→        "try_expression",
    30→        "catch_clause",
    31→        "rescue",
    32→        "except_clause",
    33→        "switch_statement",
    34→        "switch_expression",
    35→        "match_expression",
    36→        "case_clause",
    37→        "match_arm",
    38→        "with_statement",
    39→        "with_clause",
    40→        "lambda_expression",
    41→        "closure_expression",
    42→    }
    43→)
    44→
    45→
    46→def compute_nesting_depth_ts(
    47→    filepath: str, spec: TreeSitterLangSpec, parser, language
    48→) -> int | None:
    49→    """Compute max control-flow nesting depth via iterative AST walk."""
    50→    del language
    51→    cached = _PARSE_CACHE.get_or_parse(filepath, parser, spec.grammar)
    52→    if cached is None:
    53→        return None
    54→    _source, tree = cached
    55→
    56→    max_depth = 0
    57→    stack: list[tuple[object, int]] = [(tree.root_node, 0)]
    58→    while stack:
    59→        node, depth = stack.pop()
    60→        if node.type in _NESTING_NODE_TYPES:
    61→            depth += 1
    62→            if depth > max_depth:
    63→                max_depth = depth
    64→        for index in range(node.child_count - 1, -1, -1):
    65→            stack.append((node.children[index], depth))
    66→    return max_depth
    67→
    68→
    69→def make_nesting_depth_compute(spec: TreeSitterLangSpec) -> ComputeFn:
    70→    """Build a complexity compute callback for max nesting depth."""
    71→    _cached_parser: dict[str, Any] = {}
    72→
    73→    def compute(content: str, lines: list[str], *, _filepath: str = "") -> tuple[int, str] | None:
    74→        del content, lines
    75→        if not _filepath:
    76→            return None
    77→        if not _ensure_parser(_cached_parser, spec):
    78→            return None
    79→
    80→        depth = compute_nesting_depth_ts(
    81→            _filepath,
    82→            spec,
    83→            _cached_parser["parser"],
    84→            _cached_parser["language"],
    85→        )
    86→        if depth is None or depth <= 0:
    87→            return None
    88→        return depth, f"nesting depth {depth}"
    89→
    90→    return compute
    91→
```

```json
  "content": "\"\"\"Dict key flow analysis — detect dead writes, phantom reads, typos, and schema drift.\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport ast\nimport importlib\nimport logging\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfrom desloppify.base.discovery.paths import get_project_root\nfrom desloppify.base.discovery.source import find_py_files\n\nfrom .schema import detect_schema_drift\nfrom .shared import (\n    TrackedDict,\n    _BULK_READ_METHODS,\n    _CONFIG_NAMES,\n    _READ_METHODS,\n    _WRITE_METHODS,\n    _get_name,\n    _get_str_key,\n    _is_singular_plural,\n    _levenshtein,\n)\n\nlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n\n\ndef _load_dict_key_visitor():\n    module = importlib.import_module(\".visitor\", package=__package__)\n    return module.DictKeyVisitor\n\n\ndef detect_dict_key_flow(path: Path) -> tuple[list[dict], int]:\n    \"\"\"Walk all .py files and run DictKeyVisitor. Returns (issues, files_checked).\"\"\"\n    dict_key_visitor = _load_dict_key_visitor()\n    files = find_py_files(path)\n    all_issues: list[dict] = []\n\n    for filepath in files:\n        try:\n            file_path = (\n                Path(filepath) if Path(filepath).is_absolute() else get_project_root() / filepath\n            )\n            source = file_path.read_text()\n        except (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError) as exc:\n            logger.debug(\n                \"Skipping unreadable python file %s in dict-key pass: %s\",\n                filepath,\n                exc,\n            )\n            continue\n\n        try:\n            tree = ast.parse(source, filename=filepath)\n        except SyntaxError as exc:\n            logger.debug(\n                \"Skipping unparseable python file %s in dict-key pass: %s\",\n                filepath,\n                exc,\n            )\n            continue\n\n        visitor = dict_key_visitor(filepath)\n        visitor.visit(tree)\n        all_issues.extend(visitor._issues)\n\n    return all_issues, len(files)\n\n\n__all__ = [\n    \"TrackedDict\",\n    \"detect_dict_key_flow\",\n    \"detect_schema_drift\",\n]\n"
}
```
